- **Authors**: Teemu Paloniemi, Manu Setälä, Tommi Mikkonen
- **Abstract**: Given the data-intensive nature of Machine Learning (ML) systems in general, and Large Language Models (LLM) in particular, using them in cloud based environments can become a challenge due to legislation related to privacy and security of data. Taking such aspects into consideration implies porting the LLMs to an on-premise environment, where privacy and security can be controlled. In this paper, we study this porting process of a real-life application using ChatGPT, which runs in a public cloud, to an on-premise environment. The application being ported is AIPA, a system that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) and sophisticated data analytics to enhance the assessment of procurement call bids. The main considerations in the porting process include transparency of open source models and cost of hardware, which are central design choices of the on-premise environment. In addition to presenting the porting process, we evaluate downsides and benefits associated with porting.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can do that. Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Porting an LLM based Application from ChatGPT to an On-Premise Environment":

**Summary:**

The paper explores the process of migrating an application that relies on a cloud-based Large Language Model (LLM) – specifically ChatGPT – to an on-premise environment. The application in question is AIPA (Artificial Intelligence Procurement Assistant), which matches company profiles to relevant public procurement bids.  The paper outlines the necessary steps, including code refactoring, hardware specification, model selection, implementation (local API development, model fine-tuning), and deployment. It addresses key considerations such as data privacy, security, and cost-effectiveness when moving from a public cloud service to an on-premise setup. The authors also discuss the tradeoffs between model size, performance, and available computing resources. The paper finds that porting is feasible and can address privacy and cost concerns, even if it involves some loss of performance that can be mitigated through fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles a relevant and timely issue: the tension between leveraging powerful cloud-based LLMs and the growing need for data privacy and control, especially in light of regulations like GDPR and the EU AI Act. The choice of AIPA as a case study grounds the research in a practical application, making the findings more tangible.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Relevance:** The paper addresses a real-world problem that many organizations are facing: how to balance the benefits of LLMs with the need to comply with data privacy regulations.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper outlines a structured approach to porting, making it easier for others to replicate the process.
    *   **Addressing Key Considerations:** It explicitly tackles important design decisions, such as open-source model selection, hardware specifications, and the tradeoff between accuracy and computational resources.
    *   **Open-source and Transparency focus:** The work highlights an increasingly important aspect for many organizations.
    *   **Industry relevance**: Demonstrating the possibility of running such a system in the real-world is clearly relevant.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation of the ported system seems somewhat superficial. While the authors mention testing by human users, there's a lack of quantitative comparison with the original ChatGPT-based AIPA in terms of accuracy, speed, or other performance metrics. A more rigorous benchmark comparison would significantly strengthen the findings.
    *   **Model Fine-Tuning Depth:** The explanation of the fine-tuning process is relatively brief. More detail on the specific data used for fine-tuning, the training parameters, and the evaluation of the fine-tuned model's performance would be beneficial. It would be especially important to consider any degradation of performance when fine-tuning with local data.
    *   **Generalizability:** While the case study approach is valuable, it's important to acknowledge that the specific findings may not be directly generalizable to all LLM-based applications or all on-premise environments. The paper could benefit from a discussion of the factors that might influence the applicability of the proposed approach in different contexts.
    *   **Lack of Novelty:** The technical steps described are fairly standard porting and fine-tuning procedures. While the combination of these steps in this specific context is useful, the individual steps are not particularly novel.
    *   **Limited discussion of architectural implications**: The authors do not investigate how to modify the architecture to best leverage local computation for this type of application.
    *   **Limited evaluation of security**: Although it's stated that the porting process achieved similar levels of security, it is not quantified, nor it is detailed how this was tested.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper's primary contribution lies in providing a practical guide for porting an LLM-based application to an on-premise environment. While the individual techniques used (model selection, fine-tuning, etc.) are not groundbreaking, the paper synthesizes these techniques into a coherent process and demonstrates its applicability in a real-world scenario. The focus on data privacy and cost considerations adds to the paper's relevance and makes it a valuable resource for organizations exploring on-premise LLM deployments. However, the lack of rigorous quantitative evaluation and the limited discussion of architectural implications somewhat diminishes the paper's overall impact.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper is a worthwhile contribution to the field due to its practicality and relevance to current industry trends. However, the lack of a more rigorous evaluation and the limited novelty of the techniques used prevent it from being considered a truly exceptional work. It is a solid piece of applied research that addresses a practical problem, but could be significantly improved with a more in-depth analysis and quantitative comparison.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### GenEAva: Generating Cartoon Avatars with Fine-Grained Facial Expressions from Realistic Diffusion-based Faces
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07945v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Yu, Rupayan Mallick, Margrit Betke, Sarah Adel Bargal
- **Abstract**: Cartoon avatars have been widely used in various applications, including social media, online tutoring, and gaming. However, existing cartoon avatar datasets and generation methods struggle to present highly expressive avatars with fine-grained facial expressions and are often inspired from real-world identities, raising privacy concerns. To address these challenges, we propose a novel framework, GenEAva, for generating high-quality cartoon avatars with fine-grained facial expressions. Our approach fine-tunes a state-of-the-art text-to-image diffusion model to synthesize highly detailed and expressive facial expressions. We then incorporate a stylization model that transforms these realistic faces into cartoon avatars while preserving both identity and expression. Leveraging this framework, we introduce the first expressive cartoon avatar dataset, GenEAva 1.0, specifically designed to capture 135 fine-grained facial expressions, featuring 13,230 expressive cartoon avatars with a balanced distribution across genders, racial groups, and age ranges. We demonstrate that our fine-tuned model generates more expressive faces than the state-of-the-art text-to-image diffusion model SDXL. We also verify that the cartoon avatars generated by our framework do not include memorized identities from fine-tuning data. The proposed framework and dataset provide a diverse and expressive benchmark for future research in cartoon avatar generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GenEAva: Generating Cartoon Avatars with Fine-Grained Facial Expressions from Realistic Diffusion-based Faces":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GenEAva, a novel framework for generating high-quality cartoon avatars with fine-grained facial expressions.  It addresses the limitations of existing cartoon avatar datasets and generation methods, which often lack expressive avatars and raise privacy concerns due to identity memorization. GenEAva utilizes a fine-tuned text-to-image (T2I) diffusion model (SDXL) to synthesize detailed realistic facial expressions, which are then transformed into cartoon avatars using a stylization model (DCTNet). The paper also introduces GenEAva 1.0, a new dataset of 13,230 diverse cartoon avatars across 135 facial expression categories. The framework's performance is evaluated based on expression accuracy, identity uniqueness, and stylization effectiveness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a reasonably novel pipeline for cartoon avatar generation. The key strengths contributing to novelty are:

    *   **Fine-grained Expression Generation:** The focus on generating a diverse range of 135 facial expressions is a step forward compared to many existing datasets and models that primarily deal with basic emotions. Fine-tuning the SDXL model for this specific task is a notable contribution.
    *   **Privacy-focused Approach:** Addressing the identity memorization problem by explicitly evaluating and mitigating it through analysis against existing datasets (CelebA) and User Studies, is an important aspect. This is a relevant concern given the potential for generative models to replicate real-world identities.
    *   **Dataset Generation:** The creation of GenEAva 1.0 dataset is useful to the community, as it fulfills the need for fine-grained facial expression datasets.
    * The decision to apply the process on realistic faces instead of cartoon faces directly may or may not be a better approach in general, but at least it is a novel one that the community can evaluate.

*   **Significance:** The paper contributes to the field by providing a framework and a dataset that can be used for various applications involving cartoon avatars, such as social media, online tutoring, and gaming.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper employs both quantitative metrics (CLIP, DINO, LPIPS, Expression Error) and user studies to thoroughly evaluate the generated avatars, addressing facial expression accuracy, identity preservation, and stylization quality. The focus on identity memorization is crucial and well-addressed.
    *   **Diversity and Balance:**  The dataset design and prompts creation targeting balanced representation across genders, age ranges, and racial groups are commendable, promoting fairness and inclusivity.
    *   **Qualitative Comparison:**  The comparison with ChatGPT/DALL-E highlights the superior ability of GenEAva in capturing subtle expressions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Existing Models:** While the fine-tuning and combination are novel, the core components are based on pre-existing models (SDXL and DCTNet). The extent of innovation is therefore somewhat limited.
    *   **Stylization Model Choice:** While DCTNet is a good baseline, the paper could have explored or compared against other stylization models for possibly better aesthetic results or stronger identity preservation.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** The paper lacks ablation studies to demonstrate the impact of individual components of the framework, like the expression loss during fine-tuning, which would enhance the analysis.

* **Potential influence:**
The dataset can be very helpful for downstream tasks, and the framework provides some guidance on how to build a fine-grained expressive face dataset, so the paper is potentially impactful on the community.

*   **Overall:** The paper presents a valuable contribution, but its reliance on existing models caps the degree of novelty.  While the evaluation is comprehensive, it would be more robust with ablation studies. The framework addresses the growing need for expressive and privacy-aware avatar generation.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**  The paper offers a useful pipeline and a valuable dataset for cartoon avatar generation with fine-grained expressions. The novelty lies primarily in the combination of existing components and the specific application to expressive avatars, along with careful consideration of privacy. The strength lies in the thorough evaluation. The reliance on existing models, the lack of ablation studies, and the scope for exploring alternative stylization methods limit the overall impact to some extent, but it is still a substantial contribution.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### VCR-Bench: A Comprehensive Evaluation Framework for Video Chain-of-Thought Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07956v1)
- **Authors**: Yukun Qi, Yiming Zhao, Yu Zeng, Xikun Bao, Wenxuan Huang, Lin Chen, Zehui Chen, Jie Zhao, Zhongang Qi, Feng Zhao
- **Abstract**: The advancement of Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning has significantly enhanced the capabilities of large language models (LLMs) and large vision-language models (LVLMs). However, a rigorous evaluation framework for video CoT reasoning remains absent. Current video benchmarks fail to adequately assess the reasoning process and expose whether failures stem from deficiencies in perception or reasoning capabilities. Therefore, we introduce VCR-Bench, a novel benchmark designed to comprehensively evaluate LVLMs' Video Chain-of-Thought Reasoning capabilities. VCR-Bench comprises 859 videos spanning a variety of video content and durations, along with 1,034 high-quality question-answer pairs. Each pair is manually annotated with a stepwise CoT rationale, where every step is tagged to indicate its association with the perception or reasoning capabilities. Furthermore, we design seven distinct task dimensions and propose the CoT score to assess the entire CoT process based on the stepwise tagged CoT rationals. Extensive experiments on VCR-Bench highlight substantial limitations in current LVLMs. Even the top-performing model, o1, only achieves a 62.8% CoT score and an 56.7% accuracy, while most models score below 40%. Experiments show most models score lower on perception than reasoning steps, revealing LVLMs' key bottleneck in temporal-spatial information processing for complex video reasoning. A robust positive correlation between the CoT score and accuracy confirms the validity of our evaluation framework and underscores the critical role of CoT reasoning in solving complex video reasoning tasks. We hope VCR-Bench to serve as a standardized evaluation framework and expose the actual drawbacks in complex video reasoning task.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "VCR-Bench: A Comprehensive Evaluation Framework for Video Chain-of-Thought Reasoning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VCR-Bench, a new benchmark designed to comprehensively evaluate the video Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning capabilities of Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs). VCR-Bench consists of 859 videos and 1034 question-answer pairs, each manually annotated with stepwise CoT rationales. The benchmark includes seven distinct task dimensions covering various aspects of video understanding, and each CoT rationale step is tagged as either pertaining to perception or reasoning. The authors propose a CoT score to assess the entire reasoning process based on the stepwise CoT rationales.  They conducted extensive experiments to highlight limitations in current LVLMs, showing even the best models struggle.  They found a strong correlation between CoT score and accuracy and hope VCR-Bench serves as a standardized evaluation framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in introducing a *comprehensive benchmark specifically for video CoT reasoning*. While there are existing video understanding benchmarks, VCR-Bench distinguishes itself by: 1) providing detailed stepwise CoT annotations; 2) tagging each step as perception- or reasoning-related; and 3) creating a multi-dimensional framework to cover a broad range of video content and durations. The proposal of a CoT score, assessing the reasoning *process* and not just the final answer, also adds a layer of novelty.
*   **Significance:** The benchmark addresses a significant gap in the field. The lack of rigorous evaluation frameworks for video CoT reasoning is hindering the development of better LVLMs. VCR-Bench can help researchers better understand the limitations of existing models (particularly the bottleneck in spatiotemporal information processing) and develop more effective approaches. The strong correlation found between the proposed CoT score and accuracy suggests the metric is a viable way to assess and improve the reasoning abilities of models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive dataset:** The dataset seems well-curated, diverse, and underwent rigorous manual annotation and quality control.
    *   **Detailed annotations:** The CoT annotations and perception/reasoning tags provide a granular view of model performance.
    *   **Well-defined evaluation framework:** The proposed CoT score is innovative and directly addresses the shortcoming of solely relying on final answer accuracy.
    *   **Thorough experiments:** Evaluating a wide range of existing LVLMs on VCR-Bench provides valuable insights.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper clearly describes the benchmark, evaluation methodology, and experimental results.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity of CoT evaluation:** Automated evaluation of CoT steps using another LLM (GPT4o) introduces a dependency on the evaluator's own capabilities and potential biases. While the paper mentions using prompts to minimize this effect, it remains a concern. The use of GPT4o for this seems circular and could introduce its own errors.
    *   **Limited scope:** While covering various video types, the benchmark is still a snapshot. Future work might explore more complex or specialized video domains. The TSG portion specifically suffers from overall low scores which may make it a weaker benchmark for that particular field.
    *   **Potential bias in task definition:** The definition of the seven task dimensions, while comprehensive, is still subjective to some extent and may inadvertently bias evaluation.

*   **Potential Influence:** VCR-Bench has the potential to become a widely adopted benchmark in the video understanding and reasoning community. It could drive research towards developing LVLMs with stronger spatiotemporal reasoning capabilities and encourage a greater focus on evaluating the reasoning *process* rather than just the final answer. The detailed analysis provided by the benchmark could inspire new architectures and training techniques.

*Justification for Score:*

The paper makes a solid contribution by addressing a crucial need for a rigorous evaluation framework in video CoT reasoning. The benchmark is comprehensive, well-annotated, and provides valuable insights into current LVLM capabilities. While the automated CoT evaluation introduces potential bias, the thorough experiments and the correlation analysis with accuracy lend credibility to the results. The paper is well-written and likely to have a significant impact on the field.

### MM-IFEngine: Towards Multimodal Instruction Following
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07957v1)
- **Authors**: Shengyuan Ding, Shenxi Wu, Xiangyu Zhao, Yuhang Zang, Haodong Duan, Xiaoyi Dong, Pan Zhang, Yuhang Cao, Dahua Lin, Jiaqi Wang
- **Abstract**: The Instruction Following (IF) ability measures how well Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) understand exactly what users are telling them and whether they are doing it right. Existing multimodal instruction following training data is scarce, the benchmarks are simple with atomic instructions, and the evaluation strategies are imprecise for tasks demanding exact output constraints. To address this, we present MM-IFEngine, an effective pipeline to generate high-quality image-instruction pairs. Our MM-IFEngine pipeline yields large-scale, diverse, and high-quality training data MM-IFInstruct-23k, which is suitable for Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT) and extended as MM-IFDPO-23k for Direct Preference Optimization (DPO). We further introduce MM-IFEval, a challenging and diverse multi-modal instruction-following benchmark that includes (1) both compose-level constraints for output responses and perception-level constraints tied to the input images, and (2) a comprehensive evaluation pipeline incorporating both rule-based assessment and judge model. We conduct SFT and DPO experiments and demonstrate that fine-tuning MLLMs on MM-IFInstruct-23k and MM-IFDPO-23k achieves notable gains on various IF benchmarks, such as MM-IFEval (+10.2$\%$), MIA (+7.6$\%$), and IFEval (+12.3$\%$). The full data and evaluation code will be released on https://github.com/SYuan03/MM-IFEngine.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "MM-IFEngine: Towards Multimodal Instruction Following" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MM-IFEngine, a pipeline for generating high-quality image-instruction pairs to improve instruction following in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs).  The authors address the scarcity of training data and the simplicity of existing benchmarks in the field.  MM-IFEngine generates diverse, constraint-rich image-instruction pairs and uses them to create two new datasets: MM-IFInstruct-23k (for supervised fine-tuning, SFT) and MM-IFDPO-23k (for direct preference optimization, DPO).  The paper also presents MM-IFEval, a new multimodal instruction following benchmark with both compositional and perceptual constraints, and a hybrid evaluation system combining rule-based assessment and judge models.  Through experiments, the authors demonstrate that fine-tuning MLLMs with their datasets improves performance on instruction following tasks across several benchmarks, including their own.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper exhibits good novelty in several aspects:

*   **MM-IFEngine Pipeline:** The pipeline is a structured approach to generating multimodal instruction-following data that surpasses existing methods by incorporating explicit constraints. The idea of systematically varying and combining constraints is a valuable contribution.
*   **MM-IFEval Benchmark:** The benchmark's focus on diverse constraints (both compositional and perceptual) is a significant improvement over existing benchmarks that often have simple, atomic instructions. The hybrid evaluation scheme is also innovative, offering a more precise and robust evaluation than relying solely on LLM-as-a-judge.
*   **MM-IFInstruct/DPO Datasets:** Creating datasets tailored for both SFT and DPO is a practical and useful contribution. The method of generating negative examples for DPO through constraint removal is clever and efficient.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing a Critical Need:** Instruction following is a fundamental capability for MLLMs, and the paper directly addresses the challenges in training and evaluating this ability.  The scarcity of high-quality data and the limitations of existing benchmarks are real bottlenecks in the field.
*   **Performance Improvements:** The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed datasets and benchmark.  The gains on various benchmarks indicate that the approach generalizes well and provides a valuable resource for the community.
*   **Potential for Impact:**  The paper's contributions have the potential to significantly impact the development of more robust and reliable MLLMs.  The datasets and benchmark provide valuable tools for researchers and practitioners working on instruction following, enabling them to train and evaluate models more effectively.
*   **Hybrid Evaluation Rigor:** Addressing the issue of unreliable LLM-as-a-judge evaluations using a hybrid approach of rule-based systems for some constraints is a **very important** contribution toward reliable measurements in the field of LLMs.
*   **Limitations:**
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the types of errors made by models before and after fine-tuning with the proposed datasets.  Understanding *why* the datasets are effective would strengthen the paper's claims.
    *   While the hybrid evaluation method is a strength, the paper could include more details on the design of the rule-based verification functions and the criteria used for assessing subjective constraints with the LLM judge.
    *   The results are largely confined to one or two base models (Qwen2-VL and LLaVA). Experiments with a broader range of architectures would better demonstrate the generalizability of the approach.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-designed pipeline for generating multimodal instruction-following data, a challenging benchmark with diverse constraints, and comprehensive experiments demonstrating the effectiveness of the approach. The datasets and benchmark offer valuable resources to the research community for training and evaluating MLLMs. The hybrid approach to evaluation is a good addition to current LLM practices.

### VisualCloze: A Universal Image Generation Framework via Visual In-Context Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07960v1)
- **Authors**: Zhong-Yu Li, Ruoyi Du, Juncheng Yan, Le Zhuo, Zhen Li, Peng Gao, Zhanyu Ma, Ming-Ming Cheng
- **Abstract**: Recent progress in diffusion models significantly advances various image generation tasks. However, the current mainstream approach remains focused on building task-specific models, which have limited efficiency when supporting a wide range of different needs. While universal models attempt to address this limitation, they face critical challenges, including generalizable task instruction, appropriate task distributions, and unified architectural design. To tackle these challenges, we propose VisualCloze, a universal image generation framework, which supports a wide range of in-domain tasks, generalization to unseen ones, unseen unification of multiple tasks, and reverse generation. Unlike existing methods that rely on language-based task instruction, leading to task ambiguity and weak generalization, we integrate visual in-context learning, allowing models to identify tasks from visual demonstrations. Meanwhile, the inherent sparsity of visual task distributions hampers the learning of transferable knowledge across tasks. To this end, we introduce Graph200K, a graph-structured dataset that establishes various interrelated tasks, enhancing task density and transferable knowledge. Furthermore, we uncover that our unified image generation formulation shared a consistent objective with image infilling, enabling us to leverage the strong generative priors of pre-trained infilling models without modifying the architectures.
- **Summary**: Here's a comprehensive summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces VisualCloze, a novel image generation framework designed to handle a wide range of image generation tasks within a unified model. The core idea is to leverage visual in-context learning, where the model learns to perform a specific task by observing a few visual examples as demonstrations, rather than relying on explicit language instructions. To address the challenge of sparse task distributions in the visual domain, the authors introduce Graph200K, a graph-structured dataset designed to enhance task density and facilitate knowledge transfer between related tasks. The framework builds upon existing image infilling models, adapting them for universal image generation with minimal architectural modifications.  Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of VisualCloze across various in-domain tasks, its ability to generalize to unseen tasks, and its potential for task unification.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:* The paper presents several novel aspects:

1.  *Visual In-Context Learning for Universal Generation:* While in-context learning is gaining traction in language models, adapting it for universal image generation using *purely visual* cues is a valuable contribution. This approach addresses limitations associated with language-based instructions, particularly ambiguities and the vision-language modality gap.

2.  *Graph200K Dataset:* The construction of a graph-structured dataset, Graph200K, to increase task density and enable knowledge transfer is a significant innovation. The idea of linking different image generation tasks through shared annotations to facilitate learning is well-motivated.

3.  *Unified Framework Based on Infilling:* The realization that image infilling models provide a natural framework for universal image generation based on visual in-context learning is insightful. The method capitalizes on the strengths of pre-trained infilling models and requires minimal architectural changes, reducing training costs.

*Significance:* The potential impact of the paper is notable:

1.  *Task Agnostic Image Generation:* A universal image generation framework offers the prospect of reducing the need for task-specific models, leading to efficiency and scalability.

2.  *Generalization:* Demonstrating generalization to unseen tasks is crucial. The experiments presented showcase promising generalization capabilities.

3. *Multi-task consolidation: Demonstrated capability to consolidate multiple tasks such as depth estimation, edge detection and surface normal estimation is a notable achievement.

*Strengths:*

*   Clear Motivation: The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches and motivates the need for a universal image generation framework.
*   Technically Sound: The methodology is well-defined and builds on established techniques in diffusion models and in-context learning.
*   Comprehensive Experiments: The experiments include both qualitative and quantitative evaluations, covering a wide range of tasks and comparing against relevant baselines. The analysis of in-context learning through ablation studies is particularly useful.

*Weaknesses:*

*   Limited Scope of "Unseen" Tasks: While the paper claims generalization to unseen tasks, the differences between the training and test tasks are sometimes incremental (e.g., changing the environment or attribute modification).  A more substantial difference in the task domain would be even more compelling.
*   Dependence on Existing Models: While leveraging pre-trained infilling models is a strength in terms of efficiency, it also constrains the framework's design. A fully self-contained architecture might offer more flexibility in the long run.
*Instability in Certain Tasks: The results indicate the framework experiences instability in tasks such as object removal.

*Potential Influence:*

If the proposed approach can be scaled to more complex tasks and datasets, it could significantly influence the development of general-purpose image generation models.  The idea of visual in-context learning may inspire further research into alternative instruction mechanisms for generative models.
The Graph200K dataset is potentially valuable for the research community.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a strong combination of novelty and significance. The visual in-context learning approach, the Graph200K dataset, and the unification based on infilling models are all valuable contributions. While the scope of generalization to "unseen" tasks could be more ambitious, the paper successfully addresses several critical challenges in universal image generation. The results demonstrate the effectiveness and promise of the VisualCloze framework. Overall, the paper is an important step forward in the field of universal image generation.

### Geo4D: Leveraging Video Generators for Geometric 4D Scene Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07961v1)
- **Authors**: Zeren Jiang, Chuanxia Zheng, Iro Laina, Diane Larlus, Andrea Vedaldi
- **Abstract**: We introduce Geo4D, a method to repurpose video diffusion models for monocular 3D reconstruction of dynamic scenes. By leveraging the strong dynamic prior captured by such video models, Geo4D can be trained using only synthetic data while generalizing well to real data in a zero-shot manner. Geo4D predicts several complementary geometric modalities, namely point, depth, and ray maps. It uses a new multi-modal alignment algorithm to align and fuse these modalities, as well as multiple sliding windows, at inference time, thus obtaining robust and accurate 4D reconstruction of long videos. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks show that Geo4D significantly surpasses state-of-the-art video depth estimation methods, including recent methods such as MonST3R, which are also designed to handle dynamic scenes.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Geo4D: Leveraging Video Generators for Geometric 4D Scene Reconstruction":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Geo4D, a novel approach for monocular 4D scene reconstruction that leverages pre-trained video diffusion models. Geo4D trains solely on synthetic data, yet demonstrates strong generalization to real-world videos.  The core idea is to repurpose the dynamic priors learned by video generators for geometric understanding. Geo4D predicts multiple geometric modalities (point maps, depth maps, and ray maps) and fuses them using a new multi-modal alignment algorithm to achieve robust and accurate 4D reconstructions, even for scenes with significant object and camera motion. The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on video depth estimation and camera rotation recovery benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the *adaptation* of a pre-trained video diffusion model for a downstream geometric task (4D reconstruction). While adapting generative models for perception tasks is an emerging trend, Geo4D innovatively combines this with a multi-modal geometric representation and a dedicated fusion/alignment strategy. The concept of predicting multiple, partially redundant modalities (point, depth, and ray maps) and then fusing them is a valuable approach to improve robustness, especially in monocular settings where ambiguity is high. The explicit modeling of uncertainty within the geometric maps and incorporating it into the alignment process is another notable contribution. The novelty of individual components (video diffusion backbones, point map representations) is lower, but the *integration* and adaptation for this specific task is where the value lies.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a challenging problem - monocular 4D scene reconstruction - which has broad applications in robotics, computer graphics, and video understanding.  The zero-shot transfer from synthetic to real data is a significant result, demonstrating the potential of leveraging powerful generative models for tasks where real-world training data is scarce or difficult to acquire.  Outperforming state-of-the-art video depth estimation and camera pose estimation methods solidifies the practical importance of Geo4D. The proposed method's ability to handle scenes with extreme motion, as well as reflections, is also significant. Furthermore, providing a framework that combines multiple complimentary representations can inspire new research directions in this field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The paper presents comprehensive experimental results on multiple benchmarks, demonstrating significant improvements over existing methods.
    *   **Well-Defined Architecture:** The Geo4D architecture is clearly explained, including the multi-modal prediction, video conditioning, and the multi-modal alignment algorithm.
    *   **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies effectively isolate the impact of key design choices, such as the different geometric modalities, the multi-modal alignment strategy, and the temporal sliding window stride.
    *   **Handles Difficult Scenarios:** Geo4D handles scenarios with substantial dynamic motion and complex visual effects like reflections, which often challenge existing reconstruction methods.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Model:** The performance of Geo4D is inherently tied to the quality of the pre-trained video diffusion model.  Future improvements in video generation could directly translate to better Geo4D performance, but also limits its independent progress.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Although the inference is faster than MonST3R, the computational demands of diffusion models are still relatively high. This limits its real-time applicability.
    *   **Limited Qualitative Data** More data including a wider range of scenarios would serve as a more well-rounded assessment of the quality of the model.

*   **Potential Influence:** Geo4D has the potential to influence the field by:

    *   Encouraging further research into leveraging generative models for geometric perception tasks.
    *   Promoting the use of multi-modal geometric representations for robust 3D reconstruction.
    *   Inspiring the development of new alignment and fusion techniques for combining information from different modalities.
    *   Providing a strong baseline for future research in monocular 4D scene reconstruction.

*   **Justification of Score:** While Geo4D builds upon existing techniques (video diffusion, point map representations), it presents a novel and effective combination of these ideas within a well-designed architecture and achieves state-of-the-art results. The zero-shot transfer capability is particularly valuable and highlights the power of leveraging generative priors. However, the dependence on a pre-trained model and the computational cost limit its immediate impact. Therefore, a score of 8 is warranted, indicating a significant and impactful contribution to the field, with room for further development and optimization.

**Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### GLUS: Global-Local Reasoning Unified into A Single Large Language Model for Video Segmentation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07962v1)
- **Authors**: Lang Lin, Xueyang Yu, Ziqi Pang, Yu-Xiong Wang
- **Abstract**: This paper proposes a novel framework utilizing multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) for referring video object segmentation (RefVOS). Previous MLLM-based methods commonly struggle with the dilemma between "Ref" and "VOS": they either specialize in understanding a few key frames (global reasoning) or tracking objects on continuous frames (local reasoning), and rely on external VOS or frame selectors to mitigate the other end of the challenge. However, our framework GLUS shows that global and local consistency can be unified into a single video segmentation MLLM: a set of sparse "context frames" provides global information, while a stream of continuous "query frames" conducts local object tracking. This is further supported by jointly training the MLLM with a pre-trained VOS memory bank to simultaneously digest short-range and long-range temporal information. To improve the information efficiency within the limited context window of MLLMs, we introduce object contrastive learning to distinguish hard false-positive objects and a self-refined framework to identify crucial frames and perform propagation. By collectively integrating these insights, our GLUS delivers a simple yet effective baseline, achieving new state-of-the-art for MLLMs on the MeViS and Ref-Youtube-VOS benchmark. Our project page is at https://glus-video.github.io/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GLUS: Global-Local Reasoning Unified into A Single Large Language Model for Video Segmentation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GLUS, a framework that unifies global and local reasoning within a single multimodal large language model (MLLM) for referring video object segmentation (RefVOS).  GLUS addresses the "Ref" vs. "VOS" dilemma by employing "context frames" for global understanding and "query frames" for local object tracking. This framework is enhanced by training the MLLM with a pre-trained video object segmentation (VOS) memory bank. Additionally, the paper introduces object contrastive learning to distinguish hard false-positive objects and a self-refined framework to identify crucial frames and propagate segmentation. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art results on MeViS and Ref-YouTube-VOS benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the unified global-local reasoning approach within *a single* MLLM. Prior work often resorted to external modules or separate models for global context or local tracking, increasing complexity. GLUS's division of frames into context and query sets is a relatively simple yet effective way to adapt MLLMs to video segmentation. The integration of a pre-trained VOS memory bank and joint training with MLLM in an end-to-end manner is also novel and contributes to more robust temporal reasoning.

    The object contrastive loss contributes to enhancing fine-grained object matching by discriminating confused objects, which might be visually similar to the target object. Additionally, the key frame selection method is refined using pseudo-labels generated by the GLUS model itself, potentially enhancing the accuracy of the selection process.

*   **Significance:** RefVOS is a challenging task, and improving MLLM performance directly addresses limitations in video understanding and reasoning. GLUS simplifies the RefVOS pipeline compared to previous methods that relied on external VOS modules or frame selectors, which adds significance. Achieving state-of-the-art results on challenging datasets like MeViS validates the approach and indicates a tangible advance in performance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges in RefVOS and the limitations of existing MLLM-based approaches.
    *   **Simple and Effective Framework:** GLUS's architecture is relatively simple to implement, yet it effectively addresses the core challenges of RefVOS.
    *   **End-to-End Training:** The end-to-end training approach eliminates the need for separate training stages for VOS and MLLM, which leads to a better-optimized performance.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper provides compelling experimental results, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance on established benchmarks.
    *   **Comprehensive Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies thoroughly evaluate the contribution of each component of the GLUS framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the approach is novel in its integration, the individual components (e.g., frame sampling, memory banks, contrastive learning) are not entirely new. The value lies in how they are combined.
    *   **Dependence on Pre-trained Models:** GLUS relies on pre-trained MLLMs and VOS models, so the performance of GLUS is inherently bounded by the capabilities of the components.
    *   **Limited Computational Resources:** The experiments are limited by computational resources, and the authors mention the effect of longer context windows and increasing data would likely improve the result.

*   **Potential Impact:** GLUS offers a strong baseline for future research in MLLM-based video segmentation and reasoning. Its modular design could be easily extended with new techniques for video understanding or memory management. The pseudo labeling approach for keyframe selection offers a way of improving MLLMs’ performance and reducing manual labeling efforts.

**Justification for the Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I assign GLUS a score of **7**.

The paper addresses an important problem in video understanding with a relatively simple and effective framework. The integration of global and local reasoning is a valuable contribution, and the experimental results are compelling. The use of pre-trained models and incremental novelty prevents a higher score, it is not as groundbreaking as inventing a fully novel method. Overall, GLUS presents a significant improvement over existing methods and offers a solid foundation for future research in MLLM-based video understanding.

### PixelFlow: Pixel-Space Generative Models with Flow
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07963v1)
- **Authors**: Shoufa Chen, Chongjian Ge, Shilong Zhang, Peize Sun, Ping Luo
- **Abstract**: We present PixelFlow, a family of image generation models that operate directly in the raw pixel space, in contrast to the predominant latent-space models. This approach simplifies the image generation process by eliminating the need for a pre-trained Variational Autoencoder (VAE) and enabling the whole model end-to-end trainable. Through efficient cascade flow modeling, PixelFlow achieves affordable computation cost in pixel space. It achieves an FID of 1.98 on 256$\times$256 ImageNet class-conditional image generation benchmark. The qualitative text-to-image results demonstrate that PixelFlow excels in image quality, artistry, and semantic control. We hope this new paradigm will inspire and open up new opportunities for next-generation visual generation models. Code and models are available at https://github.com/ShoufaChen/PixelFlow.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PixelFlow: Pixel-Space Generative Models with Flow":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PixelFlow, a generative model family that operates directly in pixel space, unlike the prevalent latent-space diffusion models (LDMs). By eliminating the need for a pre-trained VAE, PixelFlow simplifies the image generation process and allows for end-to-end training. The model uses a cascaded flow modeling approach to manage the computational demands of working directly in pixel space. It starts generating images at lower resolutions and gradually upscales them. The paper showcases PixelFlow's performance on ImageNet class-conditional image generation and demonstrates its qualitative capabilities in text-to-image generation, highlighting its visual fidelity, artistry, and semantic control.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the end-to-end pixel-space generation paradigm, coupled with a cascaded flow approach. While pixel-space generation isn't entirely new (e.g., earlier diffusion models), the combination with efficient flow modeling and a Transformer architecture to achieve competitive performance, especially with the elimination of a VAE, contributes to a novel approach. The claim of simplicity is also noteworthy. However, the overall architecture is primarily built on established components. The combination, therefore, presents a incremental, yet significant, advance.

*   **Significance:** By removing the VAE component and simplifying the generation pipeline, PixelFlow offers potential advantages in terms of training efficiency and holistic diagnosis. The competitive performance on ImageNet, achieving an FID of 1.98 on the 256x256 class-conditional ImageNet benchmark, lends credibility to the approach. The promising text-to-image results further support the significance of the model. However, the paper acknowledges the computational intensity of the final resolution stage, implying areas for future improvements. Also, this opens to the potential for future generative models to operate directly on pixel space.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   End-to-end trainability: A distinct advantage compared to LDM's decoupled training approach.
    *   Competitive performance: The results presented on ImageNet and text-to-image benchmarks are compelling.
    *   Simplified architecture: Eliminating the VAE makes the model easier to understand and potentially debug.
    *   Qualitative Results: Excellent qualitative examples demonstrating its performance.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Computational cost: As the authors acknowledge, the final stage of generation still requires full-resolution attention, incurring a significant computational burden.
    *   Training convergence: The paper mentions that training convergence slows with longer sequence lengths, indicating scalability issues.
    *   Limited architecture exploration: While the Transformer-based architecture is well-established, the paper doesn't explore alternative network architectures designed specifically for pixel-space generation.
    *   Incremental Advance: While the novelty is there, it could be argued that the changes applied are more of an incremental adaptation.

*   **Potential Impact:** PixelFlow's potential impact lies in inspiring new research directions in visual generation. It challenges the dominance of LDMs and encourages the exploration of end-to-end, pixel-space models. Future work may focus on addressing the computational bottlenecks and improving scalability, further solidifying the pixel-space approach as a viable alternative. Its contribution will also be felt in future generative models.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a compelling case for pixel-space generative modeling through a simplified, end-to-end trainable framework. While the individual components (flow modeling, Transformers) are not revolutionary, their synergistic combination to overcome the inherent challenges of pixel-space generation represents a meaningful contribution. The reported results are promising, and the qualitative examples showcase the model's potential. However, the acknowledged computational limitations and the incremental nature of the architecture prevent a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### C3PO: Critical-Layer, Core-Expert, Collaborative Pathway Optimization for Test-Time Expert Re-Mixing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07964v1)
- **Authors**: Zhongyang Li, Ziyue Li, Tianyi Zhou
- **Abstract**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Large Language Models (LLMs) suffer from severely sub-optimal expert pathways-our study reveals that naive expert selection learned from pretraining leaves a surprising 10-20% accuracy gap for improvement. Motivated by this observation, we develop a novel class of test-time optimization methods to re-weight or "re-mixing" the experts in different layers jointly for each test sample. Since the test sample's ground truth is unknown, we propose to optimize a surrogate objective defined by the sample's "successful neighbors" from a reference set of samples. We introduce three surrogates and algorithms based on mode-finding, kernel regression, and the average loss of similar reference samples/tasks. To reduce the cost of optimizing whole pathways, we apply our algorithms merely to the core experts' mixing weights in critical layers, which enjoy similar performance but save significant computation. This leads to "Critical-Layer, Core-Expert, Collaborative Pathway Optimization (C3PO)". We apply C3PO to two recent MoE LLMs and examine it on six widely-used benchmarks. It consistently improves the base model by 7-15% in accuracy and outperforms widely used test-time learning baselines, e.g., in-context learning and prompt/prefix tuning, by a large margin. Moreover, C3PO enables MoE LLMs with 1-3B active parameters to outperform LLMs of 7-9B parameters, hence improving MoE's advantages on efficiency. Our thorough ablation study further sheds novel insights on achieving test-time improvement on MoE.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces C3PO (Critical-Layer, Core-Expert, Collaborative Pathway Optimization), a novel test-time optimization method for Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Large Language Models (LLMs). It addresses the sub-optimality of expert pathways in MoEs, where naive expert selection leaves a significant accuracy gap.  C3PO re-weights or "re-mixes" experts in critical layers for each test sample based on the sample's "successful neighbors" in a reference set, using surrogate objectives like mode-finding, kernel regression, and average loss on similar samples/tasks.  The method focuses on core experts within critical layers to reduce computational cost. Experiments on OLMOE and DeepSeekMoE demonstrate consistent accuracy improvements (7-15%) over base models and outperforming other test-time learning methods like in-context learning and prompt tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The concept of test-time expert re-mixing in MoEs is fairly novel, especially the approach of using "successful neighbors" to define surrogate objectives. The identification and exploitation of "critical layers" and "core experts" to reduce computation is also a practical and valuable contribution. The algorithm itself is a combination of known techniques (kernel regression, mode finding), but their application and combination in this specific MoE context and with the efficiency-focused optimizations (critical layers, core experts) gives the paper a distinct edge. Prior test-time adaptation methods for LLMs haven't explored the optimization of expert pathways in MoEs. The core idea of leveraging routing weights for optimization is a unique perspective.

* **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to improve the efficiency and performance of MoE LLMs.  The demonstrated improvements in accuracy, while using fewer active parameters, are compelling. The fact that C3PO allows smaller MoE LLMs to outperform larger dense models strengthens the argument for MoE architectures and offers a path towards more resource-efficient deployment. The ablation studies, while focused on OLMOE, provide important insights into the roles of different layers and experts. This work effectively bridges the gap between test-time optimization and MoE-based LLMs.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly identifies the sub-optimality of expert pathways as a significant issue in MoEs.
    * **Well-defined approach:** C3PO is presented as a cohesive framework with clear steps and algorithmic details.
    * **Strong experimental results:**  The experimental results consistently demonstrate the effectiveness of C3PO across multiple benchmarks and models. The comparison to ICL, Prefix tuning, and soft prompt tuning provides a strong baseline.
    * **Efficient optimization:**  Focusing on critical layers and core experts effectively reduces computational overhead.
    * **Comprehensive ablation study:**  The ablation study provides insights into the design choices and their impact on performance.
    * **Clear writing:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited model diversity:** The primary experiments focus on two MoE models, OLMOE and DeepSeekMoE.  Testing on a wider range of MoE architectures would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    * **Neighbor selection strategy:** The neighbor selection approach relies on semantic similarity, which can be computationally expensive for very large reference sets.
    * **Reference set dependency:** The performance of C3PO relies heavily on the quality and representativeness of the reference set. The process of building and maintaining a high-quality reference set is not deeply explored.
    * **Layer ablation details:** While the layer ablation study is informative, it would be even stronger if it delved into the specific types of functions (e.g., attention vs. feedforward) implemented in these crucial layers and whether this could further guide optimization.
    * **Limited analysis of Routing Weights:** The paper claims that 71.3% of top 8 after optimizations are the same as top 8 pre-optimization. How does this affect diversity among experts and their final representations.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in several areas:
    * **Test-time adaptation for MoEs:** It opens up a new direction for research on adapting MoEs to specific tasks and samples at test time.
    * **Efficient MoE inference:**  The critical layer and core expert optimization techniques can be applied to improve the efficiency of MoE inference.
    * **Understanding MoE dynamics:** The ablation studies provide insights into the roles of different layers and experts in MoEs, which can inform future MoE architecture design.
    * **Dynamic Routing weights:** Paves new paths for exploiting the routing weights for better representations.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the novelty and significance, the paper warrants a score of 7.  The core ideas are relatively novel within the MoE literature, and the impact (performance gains, efficiency improvements) is substantial. The method is well-motivated and experimentally validated. However, the limitations regarding model diversity and reference set dependency prevent a higher score.  Expanding the validation to more diverse MoE models and addressing the scalability of neighbor selection strategies would significantly strengthen the paper.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10
